Everything you flagged is fixed, and all three lanes are merged into `zia/design-system`. All six checks pass on the merged branch: format, typecheck, lint, test, build and secrets. Nothing is pushed.

**Your UI feedback:**
- **Button heights:** both buttons were already 32px. "Close" looked smaller because its light border faded into the grey footer and its shadow was weaker. It now has a darker resting border (ink on hover) and the same shadow shape as the dark button at half strength.
- **Double borders:** a list inside a card drew its own rounded border inside the card's. It now drops its own border when it sits inside one.
- **Broken status text** ("O / K", "Runni / ng"): the status label was being squeezed by the progress bar beside it. It no longer shrinks.
- **Space inside scroll:** a scrolling area now leaves room for its scrollbar when it scrolls. Inside a dialog it also gets the same 4px inset as the header.
- **Gap between "rapporteur" and "main":** a short divider line was breaking the group's outer border. It's hidden where the buttons already share an edge.
- **Click state on the textarea:** the square inner ring is gone; the rounded box draws the focus border instead.
  - This was a bug I introduced with the earlier menu-row focus fix, which broke Quartz's own exception for controls inside a box. That's fixed too.
- **Theme menu width:** it now fits its three options, with a 128px minimum instead of 192px.

**Checked:** I confirmed these in the browser through computed styles. A button still keeps its focus ring, while menu rows and the textarea don't. The browser pane went hidden near the end, so I couldn't open the theme menu again to measure its new width.

**Acceptance status** (details in `checks.md`):
- **Pass:** 1, 3–8 and 11. The build lists `/`, `/dashboard` and `/design-system`, and the gallery uses all 51 ui files.
- **Not done yet:**
  - the screenshots (#9);
  - the motion recording and reduced-motion check (#10);
  - `/zia-review branch` (#12).
- **Yours:** Lighthouse (#13).
- **Dropped:** #2, because you chose oxlint only.

**Still open for you:** should the dropdown, select and combobox menus animate, or open instantly as the zip did?

Committed as `309cc84` and `96dd062`. Your three asks:

- **Outline vs secondary:** they were identical, so I removed `outline` from Button. Quartz has only one quiet button style anyway. All ~40 uses now use `secondary`. Badge, Item, Toggle and Sidebar keep their own `outline` variants, which do look different.
- **Card sizes:** sm, default and lg now differ in more than body padding.
  - The header and footer use the size's spacing too (12, 16 and 24px).
  - lg gets a larger title and taller header and footer.
  - The footer sits at the bottom of the card, so it doesn't float when cards in a row have different heights.
  - The last sm/lg tweak isn't built yet; I'll check it with the next build.
- **Item variants:**
  - Outline rows are now separate framed rows with gaps, no doubled lines.
  - Muted rows sit on the grey well.
  - The extra separator that doubled each row's rule is gone.
  - xs text lines up with the other sizes once the next build is in.
- **File input:** "Choose File" is now a full-height grey segment at the start of the field with a divider, instead of a floating pill.

For the activity card you liked:
- The list is now a white inset panel with rounded corners, 4px inside a grey card, with the header on the grey. That's the same layout Quartz uses for dialogs.
- Hover now darkens the card's whole edge; before, the list was painting over it.

**Still running:** the new app components for empty, error, loading and other states, built by a background agent from Quartz's patterns and our session and card data:
- Container, page header
- Empty states: no sessions, nothing due, no reviews, no search results
- Error states: session failed, load failed, not found
- Loading skeletons shaped like the real rows and cards, plus a "processing" state
- Session status and score badges (0/1/2 as words, not only colour)
- Dashboard stat row and session row

They go in `src/components/app/`, plus a new gallery section. When it reports I'll review its code, build, check it in the browser and commit.

The app-state components are done, on `/design-system` in a new "App blocks" section, and the dashboard now uses them. All six gates pass (format, typecheck, lint, test, build, secrets); nothing is pushed.

**What's in `src/components/app/`:**
- **Page frame:** `Container` and `AppPageHeader`.
- **Empty states:** `EmptyState`, plus ready-made versions:
  - no sessions yet, which links to setup;
  - no cards due today;
  - no reviews yet;
  - no search results, which echoes your search.
- **Error states:** `ErrorState`, plus versions for:
  - a session that failed to process, showing its stored error;
  - a failed load, with retry and a request code;
  - not found, with a link back to the dashboard.
- **Loading:** skeletons shaped like the real session list, stat row, decision card and review card, plus a "Queued" / "Making cards" state with an indeterminate progress bar.
- **Badges:**
  - `StatusBadge` shows Queued, Making cards, Ready or Failed.
  - `ScoreBadge` shows 2 as Explained, 1 as Partly and 0 as Missed, always as a word, never colour alone.
- **Rows:**
  - `StatRow` shows cards due today, sessions this week, and percent explained back.
  - `SessionRow` shows title, repo, relative time, status and card count. Failed rows get a red wash, and only ready rows link to their cards.

**Reused:** the dashboard page now uses `Container`, `AppPageHeader` and the no-sessions empty state. The sidebar's recent sessions use `StatusBadge`, so its labels changed from "Pending"/"Processing" to "Queued"/"Making cards".

I checked the section in light and dark on the production build.

**Your words needed** (marked `TODO(zia)` in the code):
- the score words (Explained / Partly / Missed);
- what "Explained back %" counts;
- what a learner should do when a session failed with no stored error;
- a time estimate for processing.

**Gaps in the base components, not fixed:**
- Session rows can't use client-side `next/link` navigation yet.
- The skeleton still pulses, which Quartz's rules don't allow.
- Retry buttons don't show a pending state.

To view it:
- Dashboard: http://localhost:3057/dashboard
- App blocks: http://localhost:3057/design-system#app

The dashboard pages for Phases 3 to 8 are built on sample data and committed on `zia/design-system` (`4e6c00b`). Nothing is pushed.

**Checks:**
- All six gates pass: format, typecheck, lint, test, build, secrets. Format first failed on my own sidebar edit; that's fixed.
- In the production build, I loaded 9 pages in headless Chrome: light and dark, at 375 and 1280px. All 36 loads had no console errors, no sideways scroll, and one `h1` each.
- The answer key is not in any of the new pages' browser code. It is still in the `/design-system` gallery's code, the gap I'd already flagged. The data there is the sample fixture already in the repo.

**Pages** (all at http://localhost:3057):
- **`/dashboard`:** stats, "cards due today" with Start review, and a session list. The sample session is Ready; added sample sessions show Queued, Making cards and Failed.
- **`/s/5c1e0f0a-3b8e-4c6a-9f1d-2a7b8c9d0e11`:**
  - Session header with duration, start time and card count.
  - Three decision cards, each showing:
    - why, and stated or inferred ("the model's guess");
    - the files;
    - quotes with event number and Verified/Not verified;
    - the question, with an "Explain it" button.
  - The flagged card is marked; the hidden card isn't shown.
  - The other sample ids show the processing and failed states, and an unknown id gives a 404.
- **`/review` and `/review/<card>`:**
  - The queue lists cards due, by step.
  - The quiz shows the question, an answer box (Cmd/Ctrl+Enter submits), then a score, what was missing, and the next review date.
  - The model answer is a placeholder until the grading route exists.
- **`/progress`:** "decisions you can still explain", cards by step, answers per day for 30 days, score mix, and a per-session list.
- **`/setup`:** install steps marked "coming soon", what the hook uploads, and CLI tokens.
  - Create shows a sample token once, with copy.
  - Revoke asks for confirmation first.
- **`/settings`:** profile, time zone, reminder switch, a link to tokens, and delete account. Delete stays disabled until you type your email.

Everything that saves or sends is a client-side mock marked `TODO(zia)`. None of it touches Supabase or an API yet.

**Two things to know:**
- `/s/[id]` and `/review/[cardId]` use Next 16's `instant = false` route option. Without it the dev server errors on reading the URL during prerender. The build accepts it, and it can go once the real database queries sit behind a loading boundary.
- `/s/[id]` is Kasam's phase (5) in the plan, so treat this as the starting point for agreeing the decision card's props with him.

All 60 of cubic's review comments on [Khaan25/session-coach#4](https://github.com/Khaan25/session-coach/pull/4) are handled: 53 fixed, 1 partly fixed and 6 declined with reasons. Each thread has a reply and is resolved; 0 are still open. The fixes are pushed as `2ff0145`.

**What changed, in short:**
- **Bugs:**
  - a single-value slider drew two thumbs;
  - token ids could repeat after a revoke;
  - a throwing copy callback showed as a failed copy;
  - the condensed header bar could appear over the content;
  - two loops could run forever (an empty code reference, very large chart ranges);
  - relative times were rounded wrongly;
  - queued timeline steps drew a bar;
  - negative sparkline bars overflowed;
  - a dark-mode style rule could never match.
- **Chart colours:** the colour variables were never emitted, so the progress chart drew black bars. They're now the intended blue.
- **Answer keys:** the gallery's browser code no longer contains them. It now uses a small copy of the sample data without answers, with a test that keeps it in sync. A scan of the built bundles finds no `model_answer`.
- **Accessibility:**
  - field errors are linked to their inputs;
  - the current page is announced in sidebar sub-items;
  - the chart's daily values are readable by screen readers;
  - the loading announcement is no longer held back while the region is busy;
  - status and spinner labels are fixed;
  - gallery list rows have list roles.
- **Reduced motion:** the skeleton pulse and toast pulse stop. Spinners slow to one turn every 3 seconds instead of stopping, so you can still see something is loading.
- **Dead code removed:** five unused helpers.
- **Legal drafts:** they now mention CLI tokens, the profile picture and the email-send record. The heading "what we never keep" became "what we try to remove", since secret removal isn't guaranteed. Account deletion is marked as not built yet.

**Declined, with the reason posted in each thread:**
- **Three links to `/how-it-works` and `/support`:** your Phase 1 plan accepts them until those pages exist. These are the one item you might decide differently, since visitors do land on 404s until then.
- **A blanket list role on every `Item`:** wrong for standalone, button and link rows, so the callers set it instead.
- **Duplicate ids in the condensed header bar:** fixing it means redesigning how the bar copies its actions; current callers use no ids.
- **The segmented-control count:** the screen-reader description already works there.

The partly-fixed one is a number formatter: nothing used it, so I deleted it instead of fixing its rounding.

**Checks:**

Done so far this round. Types and lint pass on everything changed. Nothing is committed or pushed yet, and I haven't opened the new pages in a browser yet.

**Ring when pressing Enter in the token dialog:**
- **Why:** Enter submits the create form, the form is replaced by the token view, and the input that had focus disappears. Base UI then moves focus to the dialog panel, the browser treats that as keyboard focus, and the global focus ring outlines the whole panel.
- **Fix:** dialog, alert dialog, menu and listbox panels no longer draw the ring. They're containers that get focus programmatically, not controls, and the controls inside keep their rings.
- **The token** now uses the same copyable block as the setup commands, with no `$` prompt.

**Toasts:**
- Creating a token: "[REDACTED]". Revoking one: "Token revoked".
- Saving settings: a promise toast ("Saving your profile", then "Profile saved", or a retry message).
- Submitting a quiz answer: a promise toast ("Scoring your answer", then "Answer scored").
- Delete account says plainly that it wasn't deleted, because deletion isn't built yet; a fake "Deleted" would mislead.
- All of them run on a shared `mockRequest` stand-in until the real API routes exist. Copy buttons still confirm in place.

**Logo and header:**
- The sidebar and the marketing nav now share one `BrandMark` (the cards icon), so the graduation cap is gone. The sidebar's Review item got its own icon so it doesn't look like the logo.
- Dashboard is out of the nav links. The header shows a Dashboard button and an avatar menu (Dashboard, Settings, Sign out), or Sign in when signed out.
- It shows the signed-in state using the sample user, which the sidebar now uses too.

**Phase 1 pages:**
- New: `/how-it-works`, `/pricing`, `/support` (7 FAQ entries), `robots.txt` and `sitemap.xml`.
- Two new env vars in the root `.env.example`: `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`.
- Please confirm the "daily email when cards are due" line on how-it-works and pricing. It comes from the planned daily cron, which has its own plan.

**SSR and Realtime:** both are already the plan. Phase 2 auth uses `@supabase/ssr`. Supabase Realtime drives live session status on `/dashboard` (Phase 4) and on `/s/[id]` while a session is processing (Phase 5). Both need auth first, and I've noted this in the harness state.

**Still to do this round:**
1. When the 404/500/login agent finishes, review its work.
2. Run all gates, re-crawl every link to confirm zero 404s, and run the pixel check.
3. Commit and push.
4. Build the session row you pick from the three options above.

I added per-IP rate limits to sign-in, sign-up and the password-reset and resend-confirmation emails, using Upstash, and pushed them to #18 (`zia/stack-7-ui-fixes`, b48a23a). They only switch on once you install Upstash Redis on the Vercel project, so I haven't been able to test that a limit actually blocks anyone.

**Limits per IP address**

| Action | Allowed |
|---|---|
| Sign-in | 10 attempts per 10 minutes |
| Sign-up | 5 per hour |
| Reset and resend emails (shared) | 5 per hour |

- Supabase's own limits still apply on top of these.
- A blocked request gets the existing "Too many attempts" message. Because the limit is per IP and not per email address, the message doesn't reveal whether an account exists.
- If Redis isn't set up, or doesn't answer within 1 second, sign-in still goes through. A Redis outage can't lock everyone out. In production, a missing setup logs one error.
- On Vercel the client can't fake its IP address. Locally every request counts as the same visitor.

**Changes**
- The new code is in `lib/auth/rate-limit.ts`, with the IP lookup in `lib/auth/client-ip.ts` (with a test). The checks are wired into `(auth)/actions.ts`.
- I added `@upstash/ratelimit` and `@upstash/redis`, and listed the new env vars in `.env.example`.
- Typecheck, lint (no new warnings) and the tests pass (55/55). In the local browser, a wrong password still shows "don't match".

**What you need to do**
1. In the Vercel dashboard, go to Marketplace, choose Upstash Redis, connect it to this project and select all environments. I didn't do this because it changes your Vercel account. It sets `KV_REST_API_URL` and `KV_REST_API_TOKEN`, which the code already reads.
2. Redeploy, then try a wrong password 11 times from one browser to confirm the limit blocks you.

**Needs you before hosted testing works**
1. Push the Supabase config to hosted: run this from the repo root, answer `y` to auth and `n` to storage.
   ```bash
   supabase config push
   ```
2. Set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` and a real `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPPORT_EMAIL` in Vercel production. Production builds fail without them.
3. Set `MODEL_PROVIDER`, `MODEL_GRADE` and the matching API key, or grading answers "Grading isn't set up yet."
4. Add the Google client ID and secret in the Supabase Google provider.
5. Merge the stack #6 through #18 in order. Merging is your call; I only merge when you tell me to.

**Info only you have**
- The legal TODOs in `content/legal`: company name, contact emails, model and email providers, how long backups keep data.

**Team decisions (each needs a new migration)**
- Remove the unused insert/delete grants on the tables only the server writes.
- Save the answer and the review update in one database operation.

**Product and UX**
- Should a card answered before it's due count? Right now four quick correct answers master it.
- Typing in the time-zone search adds to the current value instead of replacing it.

**Housekeeping**
- CodeRabbit: comment `@coderabbitai review` on #8, #14, #15, #16 and #18, one PR per hour. The free plan allows one review an hour.
- I haven't checked CI on #18 since the rate-limit commit.
- Delete the stale branches `zia/stack-4-auth`, `zia/stack-5-learning` and `zia/stack-4-data`. The permission system blocked me from deleting them.
- Commit this session's log after it ends.
- After deploying, test the rate limit: the 11th wrong password should be blocked.

Partly. The repo already logs sessions, but in a different way from this guide.

**What we have**
- The script is `.claude/log-session.py`, run by the **`SessionEnd`** hook (30 s timeout).
- It writes a prompt-and-final-response markdown log per session into `.claude-logs/`, with stats and the author in the frontmatter.
- It skips tool calls and subagent turns, and `--backfill` writes logs for older sessions.
- `.claude-logs/` is committed, not gitignored, and `CLAUDE.md` has the commit policy.

**What the guide has that we don't**
- **More hooks:** `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop` and `SubagentStop` write the log as you go. Ours only writes when the session ends, so a crashed or still-open session (this one) has no log yet.
- **Subagents:** the guide adds a section with each subagent's brief and report. Ours drops subagents.
- **Redaction in the script:** the guide redacts keys, tokens, connection strings and emails before writing. Ours relies on the pre-commit credential scan plus redacting by hand.
- **Attribution:** the guide adds the GitHub login and a masked git email. Ours records the git name only.

**Two problems with the guide's script as written**
- It picks the newest transcript by modification time instead of the `transcript_path` the hook passes in. With two sessions open in one repo, it can log the wrong session.
- It's copied from another project: its comments mention a children's app and `scripts/check-secrets.sh`, which we don't have.

The useful upgrade is to add its redaction, subagent section and the three extra hooks to our `log-session.py`, keeping our use of `transcript_path`.

The logging script works, but it has never run for a real session. When I ran it by hand on this session (output went to the scratchpad, not the repo), it wrote a 512 KB log.

**Why there are no logs**
- It only runs on the `SessionEnd` hook, and in the desktop app a session only ends when you close or archive it. Compacting the conversation doesn't count.
- Only two sessions have ever run in this project: this one, and `pr-review-comments` in another worktree. Neither has ended, so the hook never fired.
- A worktree session writes its log inside that worktree. If the worktree is deleted before the log is committed, the log is lost.

**Fix:** also run the script on `Stop` (end of every turn) and `UserPromptSubmit`. The log then stays current while you work and gets committed with each push, instead of only appearing when a session closes.

Session logging now follows the guide, and it's pushed to #18 (`zia/stack-7-ui-fixes`, 160125d).

**What changed**
- **When it runs:** the log script now also runs on `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop` and `SubagentStop`, as well as `SessionEnd`. The log updates after every prompt, turn and subagent, so it no longer waits for the session to close.
- **Redaction:** it now hides keys, tokens, passwords and emails before writing, using the guide's patterns plus our own key and token formats (OpenRouter, Groq, and our `sc_` CLI tokens).
- **Cleaner prompts:** context the app attaches to your messages and automatic notifications are dropped. Each turn keeps only its final reply, not the narration between tool calls.
- **Subagents:** a new `# Subagents` section has each subagent's brief and the report it sent back.
- It still uses the transcript path the hook passes in, unlike the guide's script, so it can't log the wrong session when two are open.
- `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/rules/secrets.md` now describe the new behaviour.

**Tested** on this session, writing to the scratchpad instead of the repo:
- 33 prompts and 51 subagent briefs logged.
- No injected context or notifications left in it.
- `check-secrets` finds nothing.
- The file is 404 KB: 63 KB for the main conversation, the rest subagents.

Once it reloads the settings, this session will start writing its log into `.claude-logs/` itself. I haven't committed that log: the rules say to read it for personal data first, and the script can't catch names or learner data.

All 7 overlay files are written in `apps/web/src/components/ui/`. oxlint reports 0 errors on them (exit 0), tsc reports no errors in them, and the `cubic-bezier(.22` grep finds nothing. tsc still has one error, in `src/lib/quartz/direction.ts:25` (a `children` prop is missing; that file is not mine). I did not run the app in a browser, so none of the motion has been checked visually.

**Shared across the files**
- Every Portal passes `container={useQuartzPortal()}`.
- Dialog, AlertDialog, Sheet and Drawer roots track open state through `useOverlayOpen` from `@/lib/quartz/overlay`. Their Content calls `useQuartzModal(open && modal===true)`, so the page behind is inert while one is open.
- Raw colours are now tokens: `bg-black/*` became `bg-overlay`, the nested-dialog dim `after:bg-foreground/5` became `after:bg-overlay`, and `z-50` became `z-(--z-overlay)`.
- I dropped every backdrop and popup blur (Quartz anti-slop rule: no glass).
- Icons are Phosphor. Every file has `"use client"`.

**Per file**
- **`dialog.tsx`**
  - Quartz: the shell look (grey `overlayShellSurface`; the header is a white card with a sticky `frame` 4px in; the footer is `frame-foot` on the grey, sticky). Title is `type-heading-xs`, description `type-body text-text-2`. The X sits in a zero-height sticky corner strip at `end-4 top-5`.
  - Zip motion kept: the Viewport wrapper, the zoom-95 in and out at 200/150ms on `ease-out-quint`, and the full nested-dialog stack.
  - Added: `DialogBody` and a `size` prop (`sm`, `default`, `lg`, mapping to the `max-w-overlay*` widths). All zip exports and props are kept.
- **`alert-dialog.tsx`**
  - Quartz: the dialog shell, with the header alone as the card and the actions on the grey. Widths are `max-w-overlay-sm`, or `max-w-popover` for `sm`. The media tile is now `rounded-soft bg-well`.
  - Zip motion kept: the zoom and the `top`/`scale` nested stacking.
  - The header is now start-aligned (the zip centred it on phones). The zip API is unchanged.
- **`sheet.tsx`**
  - Merged the zip file with the sidebar-08 one. It still uses Base UI Dialog, and `data-slot="sheet-content"` is kept, so the reduced-motion reset in globals.css still applies.
  - Quartz: the shell look, widths `w-[calc(100%-var(--space-10))] sm:w-overlay`, rounded top/bottom edges, and the X at `end-4 top-5`.
  - Zip motion kept: the full 2.5rem slide on all sides, the RTL mirroring, and the 200/150ms timing on `ease-out-quint`.
  - Added `SheetBody` (with Quartz's scroll fade), `SheetOverlay` and `SheetPortal`. Every export `sidebar.tsx` uses is kept. The X is still a direct child button, so the sidebar's `[&>button]:hidden` still hides it.
- **`drawer.tsx`**
  - Quartz has no drawer. Skin: `overlaySurface`, the backdrop is `bg-overlay`, the bleed uses `--color-page`, the grab bar uses `bg-control` with `--grab-size` and `rounded-soft`, the x-axis drawer is `--overlay` wide, and the type roles match the sheet.
  - Zip motion kept: swipe progress, snap points, stacking, the bleed, and the (.32,.72,0,1) and (.45,1.005,0,1.005) curves. The popup's (.22,1,.36,1) is now `ease-out-quint`.
  - One change to the zip classes: `origin-start` and `origin-end` are not real Tailwind classes here, so they generated no CSS and failed lint. I replaced them with `origin-left rtl:origin-right` and its mirror.
- **`popover.tsx`**
  - Quartz: `overlaySurface`, `w-popover`, `p-4 gap-3`, `type-body`; title `type-label`, description `type-body-sm`. Defaults are now sideOffset 6 and collisionPadding 16.
  - Zip motion kept: `origin-(--transform-origin)` and the slide/zoom in and out. I used it instead of Quartz's opacity-only `overlayMotion`.
  - Added `PopoverClose` and a `collisionPadding` prop.
  - Not ported: Quartz's hover preview, pin behaviour and `PopoverInfo`.
- **`tooltip.tsx`**
  - Merged with the sidebar-08 file; the exports are unchanged.
  - Quartz: the `data-surface="inverse"` chip (`rounded-soft bg-page type-label-sm max-w-(--tooltip-max) shadow-overlay`). I removed the arrow because Quartz's tooltip has none. Defaults are now sideOffset 6 and collisionPadding 8.
  - Zip motion kept unchanged, including `delayed-open`. The zip's `delay=0` default on `TooltipProvider` is kept.
  - Added the `tooltipPopupClass` export, which the toast file uses.
  - Not ported: Quartz's automatic trigger naming and the `TooltipShortcut` prop.
- **`toast.tsx`**
  - Quartz: the toast is `rounded-frame bg-page shadow-overlay` with `focus-visible:focus-ring`; content `py-3 ps-4 pe-2`; title `type-label` (fail-text or warn-text for error and warning); description `type-body-sm`. Close is ghost `icon-xs`, the action is secondary `xs`. The viewport uses the gutter tokens and `max-w-toast`. The anchored toast uses the tooltip chip look.
  - Zip motion kept: all the stacking and swipe maths. The transform transition now uses `var(--ease-out-quint)`, and the content uses `ease-out-quint`.
  - Pulse: lint rejects dynamically built class names, so the even/odd pulse now runs from a `data-pulse` attribute mapped to `animate-toast-pulse-even/odd`. The class still flips each update, so the animation re-runs as before. Title colour works the same way, through `data-toast-type`.
  - `ToastArrow` is still exported but no longer rendered in the anchored list (no arrow, to match the tooltip).
  - Not ported from Quartz: the reading-time timeout, the drain line, the limit that keeps fail toasts visible, the slide animation between stacked toasts, and its own `toast.success/fail` API. The zip's manager and API stay.

**What callers need to know**
- Content placed directly between a header and a footer in a Dialog or Sheet now sits on the grey with no padding. Wrap it in `DialogBody` or `SheetBody` to get the Quartz white card.
- Quartz's pending-action machinery is not ported (`DialogAction`, `SheetAction`, `onAction`, typed confirm).
- `Button` comes from the current repo file, which still has the base-nova styles. Footer buttons will look right once the button agent's version lands.
- The only files outside my list that I needed were read-only imports from `@/lib/quartz/{overlay,portal,utils}`.

- Zip popup motion and geometry are kept (`min-w` is the anchor width plus 28px, and `data-chips`). Quartz's caret rotation while open was added.

  - A `size` prop on `ComboboxInput`.
  - Exports `ComboboxClear` and `listMaxHeightClass`.
  - The list max height is now `min(--popup-max, available-height - --row)` instead of 252px.
- Not ported: Quartz's creatable, email and pending modes (they need Empty, Kbd and CodeTag).

**virtualized-combobox.tsx**
- The zip logic is ported with its comments and the test was skipped, as briefed.
- The default row height changed from 28 to 32, to match `menu-item`'s `--row-sm`.
- The `height` and `translateY` inline styles were rejected by `no-inline-styles`. They became CSS vars (`--total-size`, `--item-size`, `--item-start`) used by `h-(--…)` and `translate-y-(--item-start)` classes.
- The context now carries `measureElement` instead of the whole virtualizer, because the React `refs` lint rule gave a false positive on it.
- `ComboboxVirtualizedItem` now takes `className` and `style` as a plain string and object only, because the `require-static-classes` rule rejects a function className.

**breadcrumb.tsx** (no `"use client"`, icons from `@phosphor-icons/react/ssr`, all existing exports kept)
- From Quartz: `type-label text-text-2` list, links that go to ink with an underline on hover, the page in `text-text-1`, and a `/` separator in `text-3` (the CaretRight is gone).
- `transition-colors` from the zip is kept.
- Added: `BreadcrumbId`, which is pure.
- Removed: `role="link"` and `aria-disabled` from `BreadcrumbPage`, matching Quartz and the zip.
- Not ported: `BreadcrumbTrail`, because it needs hooks and the dropdown, which would make the file client-only.

**scroll-area.tsx**
- The zip structure is kept. The thumb is `bg-(--scrollbar-thumb)` with a `forced-colors` fallback, the viewport uses `scroll-fade-y` and `focus-visible:focus-ring-inset`, and the zip's `transition-colors` stays.

I did not fetch the design.how docs for each component; I relied on the Quartz source comments instead. Nothing was checked in a browser. The main visual risk is that dropdown popups no longer stretch to the trigger's width by default.

So every dynamic style Quartz had (segment shrink and min-width, timeline bar start/width/translate, guides, ticks, maxHeight, the code gutter width, the page-header sticky top) is now a CSS custom property read by a class, e.g. `start-(--bar-start)`, `w-[calc(var(--bar-end)-var(--bar-start))]`. The rendered result is the same, and the timeline's glide transitions still fire.

- **`status.tsx`** (server-safe): straight port. Exports `Status`, `StatusArc`, `InkSlot`, `statuses`, `statusLabels`, `statusRank`, `statusWordVariants`. The `motion-safe:animate-run` arc is kept.

Instead of `ChartDataTable` it uses a small inline sr-only table. There is a `shortcut:` comment in the file.
- **`chart-scale.ts`** (new): only `niceScale`, which is all Timeline needs. There is a `shortcut:` comment saying to port the rest when a chart needs it.
- **`segmented-control.tsx`**: straight port. The `#000` in the fade mask is now `var(--ink)`, to satisfy the raw-color lint. The widest-count memory moved from a ref written during render to state set during render, because the `react/refs` lint rule forbids the ref version; behaviour is the same.
- **`copy-button.tsx`**: built on the repo `Button`. The repo Button has no `pending` prop, so pending is done locally: a `CircleNotch` spinner with `motion-safe:animate-spin`, plus `disabled`, `focusableWhenDisabled` and `aria-busy`. The other glyphs are Copy, Check and Warning. The prop type is `Omit<ComponentProps<typeof Button>, ...>`.
- **`code-block.tsx`**: tokenizer, CodeBlock (file and command forms), Code and MaskedKey are ported. `maskSecret` and `keyPrefixLength` are inlined locally, without the capacity parameter. The icons for wrap and reveal are ArrowElbowDownLeft, Eye and EyeSlash. In `TokenLine`, each token's start offset is now worked out before render instead of mutated inside the map, because the `react/immutability` lint rule rejected the mutation.

I worked around it by wrapping it in `<Suspense>` in the gallery. Anyone else who uses it will hit the same error until it gets a deterministic width.
2. I didn't need any new variants.

**Not rendered, and why**
- **`Sidebar` variants `floating`/`inset` and `collapsible` `offcanvas`/`icon`:** they position themselves fixed against the viewport and would sit on top of the page. The demo uses `collapsible="none"` inside a frame.
- **`SidebarInset`, `SidebarRail`:** SidebarInset renders a `<main>`, which would become the page's landmark. SidebarRail only works with a collapsible sidebar.
- **`PageHeader size="sm"`:** a second one would put a second `h1` on the page. Only the default size is shown.
- **`CodeBlock` `TokenLine` export:** an internal helper, not rendered.
- **Toast variants:** `toast` is covered through the blocks, using the default, anchored and bottom-start managers.

I didn't check the page at phone width or in dark mode. The browser tab is shared with other lanes, so I tested in my own tab.

**Commits**

**Step 1, reference capture.** I used the REA tools: `capture_web_screenshot` over CDP against headless Chrome. I measured with the browser pane (computed styles and `document.getAnimations()`). The write-up is `docs/design/landing-hero.md`. It contains no linkletter copy, images or code.
- Screenshots:
  - `docs/design/screenshots/reference/hero-1440.png`
  - `docs/design/screenshots/reference/hero-500.png`. Headless Chrome will not open narrower than 500px, so the 375px numbers come from pane emulation.
- Nav: 80px tall (72px on phones) in a 1112px box. Four links at 16px/600 with a 32px gap. Button 46px tall, 12px radius. The links hide under the tablet breakpoint.
- Headline: serif, 40/52/60px at 375/810/1200+, line-height 1, tracking -0.02em, 680px max width, 120px below the nav (80px on phones).
- Underline: an SVG, 393×28 (280×20 on phones), pulled up 8px.
- Subhead: 16/22.4px, 360px max width. 20px above and below it, then the CTA (48px tall, 12px radius). The note under the CTA is 14/19.6px, 8px below it.
- Product image: 635×947. The trust strip has 6 logos at 70% opacity.
- Motion: the only hero motion is the underline. It wipes in with clip-path over 650ms on `cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1)`, which is our ease-out-quint, starting about 500ms after load. The trust rail fades in over 500ms ease-out. Hover transitions are 150ms. Reduced motion cuts everything to 0.01ms.

**Step 2, what I built**
- `apps/web/src/app/page.tsx` is deleted.
- `apps/web/src/app/(marketing)/layout.tsx` holds the nav, `main` and the footer.
- `apps/web/src/app/(marketing)/page.tsx` is the hero.
- `apps/web/src/components/marketing/` holds `site-nav.tsx`, `site-footer.tsx`, `hand-underline.tsx` and `decision-card-preview.tsx`.

**Decisions**
- **Copy:** only the facts you gave me.
  - Headline: "*Explain* the code your agent wrote."
  - Subhead: the site description, plus "Each session becomes 3 to 5 decision cards, due again on day 1, 3 and 7."
  - CTA: "See how it works", linking to `/how-it-works`.
  - Note under the CTA: "Built for Claude Code sessions." This comes from the old page.tsx copy and the fixture's `agent: claude-code`. A TODO(zia) marks it for the real reassurance note.
  - Trusted-by strip: left out, with a TODO(zia) comment.
- **Nav:** links to How it works, Pricing, Support and Dashboard. The button is "Sign in", linking to `/login` (taken from docs/plans/auth.md). It uses the outline variant because Quartz allows one ink primary per view, and the hero CTA is that primary. The footer links to Privacy, Terms and Support. Every planned page is marked TODO(zia) and 404s until it lands.
- **Product object:** card 0 of the sample fixture, built from Card and Badge.
  - Header: session title, repo, duration, and "Card 1 of 4".
  - Body: the decision, the why, and the evidence quote in a well with a Stated badge and the file path.
  - Footer: the question, with Day 1/3/7 badges.
  - `model_answer` is not shown, because it is the answer key.
  - Two blank Cards sit behind it, rotated ±2–3°, so it reads as a deck.
- **Colour:** ink on paper is the dominant colour. The one accent is the underline stroke in `text-accent-line` with `data-kind="run"` (Quartz run blue). I chose blue because Quartz reserves warm hues like the reference's rust for warnings and failures.
- **Background:** a full-bleed `well` band under the card stack, ruled like an index card. The rules are an inline SVG `<pattern>` stroked with `currentColor` and `text-line-row`. That makes it tokens-only but not strictly CSS-only, since repeating-gradient rules would need arbitrary values.
- **Load sequence:** tw-animate-css `animate-in fade-in slide-in-from-bottom-*` on `ease-out-quint`, with fill-mode both and `motion-reduce:animate-none`. Timing: nav at 0ms, headline at 100ms, subhead at 300ms, CTA at 500ms, card at 700ms over 1000ms.
- **Underline motion:** two hand-authored strokes drawn with SMIL `stroke-dashoffset`. `keySplines="0.22 1 0.36 1"` is the same ease-out-quint curve, starting at 600ms and lasting 650ms like the reference. A static copy shows under `motion-reduce:`. SMIL keeps this out of globals.css. One limit: if someone navigates to `/` from another page on the client, the stroke appears already drawn.
- **Type:** the h1 is `font-display text-5xl sm:text-7xl lg:text-8xl leading-none`, giving 48/72/96px against 16px body text. That covers the size jump without clamp() or arbitrary values. Everything else is Geist through Quartz `type-*` roles.
- `apps/web/public/marketing/` is unused because the underline is inline.

Lane: React/components only. I left out CSS/visual, a11y beyond the component contract, Quartz CSS, and lint/class-at-call-site rules. Every ui file with hooks has `"use client"`. Every server file uses `@phosphor-icons/react/ssr`. No barrels added. Base UI `useRender` in server files is safe: Base UI 1.9 skips the ref merge on the server. The `bg-sidebar` tokens exist in `utilities.css`.

### 1. Important: mobile sidebar Sheet stays open after a link is tapped
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: high.
- Evidence: `apps/web/src/components/nav-main.tsx:26-29` renders `render={<Link href={item.url} />}` and never calls `setOpenMobile`. `nav-secondary.tsx:26`, `nav-sessions.tsx:40` and `nav-user.tsx:57` do the same. `ui/sidebar.tsx:180-182` keeps the mobile Sheet open while `openMobile` is true, and nothing clears it on route change.
- Impact: on phones the route changes behind a modal sheet that stays open, and the page stays inert. The user has to close it by hand.
- Fix: one place in `SidebarProvider`. Add `const pathname = usePathname(); useEffect(() => setOpenMobile(false), [pathname]);`. Or wrap each nav `Link` with `onClick={() => setOpenMobile(false)}` from `useSidebar()`. The provider version covers all 4 nav files.

### 2. Important: ThemeToggle shows the wrong icon on first paint, then animates
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: high.
- Evidence: `components/theme/theme-toggle.tsx:39-45` drives both icons off `data-resolved={resolvedTheme}`. `theme-provider.tsx:100-105` uses server snapshots `() => null` and `() => false`, so the SSR and hydration render always has `resolvedTheme === "light"`.
- Impact: dark-mode users see the sun in the SSR HTML. After hydration it rotates and cross-fades to the moon (200ms) on every page load, in both the app header and the gallery. The `<head>` script already fixed the page colours, so only this control flashes.
- Fix: use the `dark:` variant instead of `data-[resolved=dark]:`. `utilities.css:10` defines `dark` as matching `.dark *`, and `themeScript` sets `.dark` before paint. For example, sun: `dark:scale-0 dark:-rotate-90 dark:opacity-0`. Then drop `data-resolved`.

### 3. Advisory: `themeScript` string is exported from a `"use client"` module and used by the server root layout
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: medium.
- Evidence: `components/theme/theme-provider.tsx:1` is `"use client"`, and `:33` has `export const themeScript = ...`. `app/layout.tsx:4` imports it, and `:41` uses `<script dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: themeScript }} />`.
- Impact: in a server component, any import from a client module is a client-reference proxy, not the string. It works only because the reference is resolved again in the SSR layer. This is fragile, and it puts the no-flash script on a client-module round trip.
- Fix: move `STORAGE_KEY`, `DARK_QUERY` and `themeScript` to a plain `components/theme/theme-script.ts` with no directive. Import it from both `layout.tsx` and `theme-provider.tsx`.

### 4. Advisory: nav data lives in a client component file, and two consumers decide the active route differently
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: medium.
- Evidence:
  - `components/app-sidebar.tsx:32` has `export const navMain = [...]` inside a `"use client"` file.
  - `app/(app)/app-breadcrumb.tsx:18` matches with `navMain.find((item) => pathname.startsWith(item.url))`.
  - `nav-main.tsx:28` matches with `pathname === item.url || pathname.startsWith(`${item.url}/`)`.
- Impact: the breadcrumb and the sidebar can disagree, for example on `/settings-x` or `/reviewer`. Any future server import of `navMain` gets a proxy, and `.find` throws.
- Fix: move `navMain` (and `navSecondary`) to a plain `components/nav-items.tsx` and add one `isActiveRoute(pathname, url)` helper. Use it in both places.

### 5. Advisory: sidebar cookie is written but never read
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: high.
- Evidence: `ui/sidebar.tsx:87` writes `document.cookie = \`${SIDEBAR_COOKIE_NAME}=${openState}...`. `app/(app)/layout.tsx:16` renders `<SidebarProvider>` with no `defaultOpen`. Nothing in `src` reads `sidebar_state`.
- Impact: the collapsed state resets on every reload, and the cookie write is dead behaviour.
- Fix: decide which you want. Either read `cookies()` in the layout (under cacheComponents this needs a Suspense or dynamic boundary, which costs the static shell), or delete the cookie write and its constants.

### 6. Advisory: Calendar formats with the runtime's default locale during SSR
- Scope: introduced by branch (new file). Confidence: medium.
- Evidence: `ui/calendar.tsx:167` has `data-day={day.date.toLocaleDateString(locale?.code)}`, and `:42` has `date.toLocaleString(locale?.code, { month: "short" })`. `catalog-inputs.tsx:507-531` renders three Calendars with no `locale`.
- Impact: with `undefined` locale, Node (en-US) and a browser in, say, en-GB produce different strings. That gives hydration attribute and text mismatches on `/design-system`, and later on any app page using Calendar.
- Fix: default `locale?.code ?? "en-US"`, or pass the date-fns locale through. Or use an ISO key for `data-day`: `format(day.date, "yyyy-MM-dd")`.

### 7. Advisory: ThemeProvider writes the server-snapshot class during the hydration commit
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: low (timing-dependent; not reproduced).
- Evidence: `theme-provider.tsx:112-116` runs `root.classList.remove("light","dark"); root.classList.add(resolvedTheme)` in a `useEffect`. On the hydration commit `resolvedTheme` is `"light"`, from the server snapshot. The uSES re-render then corrects it to `"dark"`.
- Impact: for one passive-effect pass, `<html>` has `light` while the user is dark. This is likely in the same task, so no paint. But any forced style read in between could start colour transitions.
- Fix: skip the write when `root.classList.contains(resolvedTheme)`. Or apply the class in `writeStoredTheme` and the media listener instead of an effect.

### 8. Advisory: InputGroupAddon focus forwarding misses textareas, and callers can silently override it
- Scope: introduced by branch (shadcn upstream pattern). Confidence: medium.
- Evidence: `ui/input-group.tsx:73` has `event.currentTarget.parentElement?.querySelector("input")?.focus();`. The `onMouseDown` at `:67` comes before `{...props}` at `:75`.
- Impact: clicking an addon in an `InputGroupTextarea` group does nothing. A caller passing `onMouseDown` also drops the forwarding.
- Fix: query `"[data-slot=input-group-control]"`, which both controls set. Spread `props` first, then compose: call `props.onMouseDown?.(event)` before the built-in logic.

### 9. Advisory: stale comment and an extra Suspense in the gallery
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: high.
- Evidence: `components/design-system/catalog-navigation.tsx:114` says `SidebarMenuSkeleton picks its width with Math.random()...` and wraps it in `<Suspense fallback={null}>`. But `ui/sidebar.tsx:597-600` now derives the width from `useId`.
- Fix: delete the comment and the `Suspense` wrapper.

### 10. Advisory: `nuqs` added but never used
- Scope: introduced by branch. Confidence: high.
- Evidence: `apps/web/package.json` adds `"nuqs": "2.10.2"`. There is no `nuqs` or `NuqsAdapter` import in `src`. `app/design-system/page.tsx:42` says sections are plain `#anchors`, so the page "needs no search params". DECISIONS approves nuqs for gallery section and tab state.
- Fix: remove the dependency until a real consumer exists, or update the DECISIONS line.

### 11. Not worth changing (one-line fix if touched): deprecated Phosphor alias
- Evidence: `app/(marketing)/page.tsx:1` and `components/marketing/site-nav.tsx:1` use `import { Cards } from "@phosphor-icons/react/ssr"`. In 2.1.10, `Cards` is `/** @deprecated Use CardsIcon */`. Everywhere else uses `*Icon` names.
- Fix: rename to `CardsIcon`.

### 12. Not worth changing: NavMain tooltips cannot show
- Evidence: `nav-main.tsx:27` sets `tooltip={item.title}`. `AppSidebar` uses the default `collapsible="offcanvas"`, so when collapsed the whole sidebar is off-screen. The tooltip only helps with `collapsible="icon"`.
- Cost: 5 idle Tooltip roots.
- Fix: keep it if icon-collapse is planned; otherwise drop the prop.

Files read: `app/layout.tsx`, `app/(app)/*`, `app/(marketing)/*`, `app/design-system/page.tsx`, `components/app-sidebar.tsx`, `components/nav-*.tsx`, `components/theme/*`, `components/marketing/*`, `hooks/use-mobile.ts`. Of `components/ui`: `button`, `badge` (head), `sidebar`, `tooltip`, `toast`, `dropdown-menu`, `sheet`, `tabs`, `input-group`, `page-header` (core), `calendar` (parts). Also `lib/quartz/portal.ts` and `useOverlayOpen` in `overlay.ts`.

Not checked: dialog, alert-dialog, drawer, combobox, virtualized-combobox, code-block, chart, segmented-control, timeline, and most gallery catalog bodies (I only grepped them). Nothing was run: no typecheck, lint or browser check. Findings 2, 6 and 7 come from reading the code and are not reproduced in a browser.

timestamp: 2026-10-29T10:51:42.413Z
chars: 12661

Behavior/resilience review of `git diff main...HEAD -- apps/web/src` on branch zia/design-system. Every finding below is in a file this branch adds, so all are "introduced by branch". Paths are relative to `/Users/ziaurrehmankhan/code/personal/session-coach/.claude/worktrees/design-system-landing-shell-57b973/apps/web/src/`.

**Bottom line:** I found no Blocking issue. The two most important problems are the theme failing when browser storage is blocked, and primary buttons that lead to 404 pages. No server→client prop exposes `model_answer` or hidden cards. However, the fixture JSON is bundled into client JS (finding 3), and the landing card count includes the hidden card (finding 4).

---

**1. Important: blocked storage gives dark-OS users a light flash, and the theme toggle stops working**
- **File:** `components/theme/theme-provider.tsx:33` and `:49-68`
- **Evidence:**
  - Line 33: `try{var t=localStorage.getItem(...);if(t!=="light"&&t!=="dark")t=matchMedia(...)...;document.documentElement.classList.add(t)}catch(e){}`. When `getItem` throws, `matchMedia` and `classList.add` never run.
  - The tokens define dark only through `.dark` or `[data-theme=dark]` (`quartz/tokens.css:699`). There is no `prefers-color-scheme` fallback, so first paint is light until the hydration effect runs.
  - `writeStoredTheme` catches the `setItem` failure and notifies listeners. But `readStoredTheme` is the store snapshot and still returns `null`. So `theme` stays `defaultTheme` and choosing Light or Dark does nothing.
  - The comment "the choice lasts for this page view only" is therefore false.
- **Impact:** Safari private mode, blocked third-party storage, or some embedded webviews show the wrong theme first. The toggle there is broken with no feedback.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:**
  - In the script, wrap only the `getItem` call in its own try, then always run the `matchMedia` and `classList.add` steps.
  - In the provider, keep a module-level `let memoryTheme: Theme | null = null`. Set it in `writeStoredTheme` before the try. Make `readStoredTheme` return `isTheme(stored) ? stored : memoryTheme`, and return `memoryTheme` from its catch branch too.

**2. Important: primary buttons and visible nav links go to 404 pages**
- **Files:**
  - `app/(marketing)/page.tsx:49`: hero CTA `<Link href="/how-it-works" />`, "See how it works".
  - `app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx:46`: empty-state CTA `<Link href="/setup" />`.
  - `components/app-sidebar.tsx:35-44`: `/review`, `/progress`, `/setup`, `/settings`, `/privacy`.
  - `components/nav-sessions.tsx:40`: `href={\`/s/${session.id}\`}` (no TODO there).
  - `components/nav-user.tsx:57`: `/settings`.
  - `components/marketing/site-nav.tsx:37`: `/login`.
  - `components/marketing/site-footer.tsx`: `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/support`.
- **Evidence:** `find app -type f` shows only `/`, `/dashboard`, `/design-system` and `api/*`. There is no `not-found.tsx`. Comments admit some of these 404 (`site-nav.tsx:6`, `site-footer.tsx:3`, `app-sidebar.tsx:31`). But the only CTA on the landing page and the only next step on the dashboard both dead-end.
- **Impact:** The main flow fails on the first click. In production, Next also prefetches visible Links, so each one fires a 404 request.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:**
  - For the hero and dashboard CTAs, either render a non-link disabled `Button` with a `TODO(zia)`, or point them at an existing route (for example `#` plus a TODO, or `/design-system`).
  - For the sidebar entries and `/s/:id`, add `prefetch={false}` and a TODO comment, or render them as disabled menu buttons until the routes land.

**3. Advisory: the full fixture, including `model_answer` and the hidden card, ships in the client JS of `/design-system`**
- **File:** `components/design-system/sample.ts:1,6`
- **Evidence:**
  - `import sample from "@session-coach/shared/fixtures/session.sample.json"` and `const cards = sample.cards.filter((card) => !card.hidden)`.
  - `sample.ts` is imported by "use client" modules: the six catalog files and all four `blocks/*`.
  - The filter runs at runtime, so the whole JSON is in the bundle. That includes `model_answer` for every card (fixture lines 38/62/93/117) and the `hidden: true` card (line 119).
  - Nothing renders `model_answer` (grep finds it only in a comment).
- **Impact:** This breaks the rule in `docs/rules/authorization.md` ("hides `hidden` rows and the `model_answer` column") on a public page. The data is sample data today. It becomes a real leak if this pattern is copied for real cards.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** Build the card list in a server module (`page.tsx` or a `server-only` `sample.ts`) that picks only the needed fields (`decision`, `why`, `question`, `source`, `flagged`, `evidence`, `files`) and skips hidden cards. Pass the result to the catalogs as props. Alternatively, keep a pruned client-safe fixture.

**4. Advisory: the landing card count includes the hidden card**
- **File:** `components/marketing/decision-card-preview.tsx:41`
- **Evidence:** `Card {card.position + 1} of {sample.cards.length}`. The fixture has 4 cards and one is `hidden: true`, so the landing page shows "Card 1 of 4" while a learner would see 3.
- **Impact:** Wrong copy, and it reveals that a hidden card exists. This is a server component, so no data reaches the client.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** `const visible = sample.cards.filter((c) => !c.hidden)`, and use `visible[0]` and `visible.length`.

**5. Advisory: the hydration render writes the wrong theme class, and the toggle icon starts as the sun for dark users**
- **Files:** `components/theme/theme-provider.tsx:100-116`, `components/theme/theme-toggle.tsx:37-45`
- **Evidence:**
  - Both `useSyncExternalStore` server snapshots are `null` and `false`. So the hydration render computes `resolvedTheme = "light"`.
  - The effect then runs `root.classList.remove("light","dark"); root.classList.add("light")`, overwriting the correct class that `themeScript` set. React corrects it on the forced re-render.
  - The SSR HTML of `ThemeToggle` has `data-resolved="light"`, so dark users see the sun first, then a 200ms crossfade to the moon after hydration.
- **Impact:** A visible icon swap on every load for dark users. The page class flips dark→light→dark within one task; layout effects that read layout in between (for example `useScrollFade`, `useOverflow`) can trigger colour transitions.
- **Confidence:** Medium for the page flicker; high for the icon swap.
- **Fix:**
  - Skip the effect until hydrated, for example with `const hydrated = useSyncExternalStore(noopSubscribe, () => true, () => false)` and `if (!hydrated) return;`.
  - In the toggle, hide the icon state until hydrated, or drive the icon from CSS `.dark &` instead of `data-resolved`.

**6. Advisory: the overlay pending/run machinery is unreachable**
- **Files:** `lib/quartz/overlay.ts:107-181` (`run`, `pending`), `:188-302` (`closesAfter`, `useDefaultAction`); callers `components/ui/dialog.tsx:25`, `alert-dialog.tsx:22`, `sheet.tsx:24`, `drawer.tsx`.
- **Evidence:** Every caller destructures only `{ open, setOpen }`. Grep finds no use of `run`, `pending`, `useDefaultAction`, `closesAfter` or `cycleTab` outside `lib/quartz`. So `if (!next && !overlayMayClose(...)) { full.cancel(); return; }` can never fire. Async confirms do not block Escape or the backdrop, despite the comment ("nothing closes the overlay until it settles").
- **Impact:** The comments promise a close-blocking guarantee that does not exist. Each open and close also runs an extra `setModel` re-render through the layout effect at `:130-132`.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** Either expose `run` and `pending` through a context and wire them into `AlertDialogAction` and `DialogFooter`, or use plain `useState` in the components and drop the unused exports. This is vendored code, so a TODO noting the limit is also acceptable.

**7. Advisory: the mobile sidebar sheet stays open after a client navigation**
- **Files:** `components/nav-main.tsx:29`, `nav-sessions.tsx:40`, `app-sidebar.tsx:59` (with `components/ui/sidebar.tsx` `openMobile`)
- **Evidence:** The links are plain `<Link>`. Nothing calls `setOpenMobile(false)` on click or on a pathname change. The `(app)` layout persists across navigations, so the Sheet stays open and the new page stays inert.
- **Impact:** This does not show today because the target routes 404 and unmount the layout. It will appear as soon as `/review` and the other routes land.
- **Confidence:** Medium-high.
- **Fix:** In `SidebarProvider`, add `const pathname = usePathname(); useEffect(() => setOpenMobile(false), [pathname]);`, or add `onClick={() => isMobile && setOpenMobile(false)}` on the menu links.

**8. Advisory: the `sidebar_state` cookie is written but never read**
- **Files:** `components/ui/sidebar.tsx:87`, `app/(app)/layout.tsx:16`
- **Evidence:** `document.cookie = \`${SIDEBAR_COOKIE_NAME}=${openState}...\``. `<SidebarProvider>` is rendered without `defaultOpen` from `cookies()`. The gallery's own SidebarProvider (`catalog-navigation.tsx`) also writes the same cookie.
- **Impact:** The collapsed state is lost on every reload. The gallery also overwrites the app's preference.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** Either remove the write, or read the cookie in the `(app)` layout. Under `cacheComponents`, `cookies()` makes the layout dynamic, so it needs a Suspense boundary. A cheaper option is to read it on the client in an effect.

**9. Advisory: the dashboard says "No sessions yet" while the sidebar lists a session**
- **Files:** `app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx:39`, `app/(app)/layout.tsx:12`
- **Evidence:** The layout passes `[sessionSchema.parse(sample.session)]` to "Recent sessions", while the page shows "No sessions yet … once the CLI is set up".
- **Impact:** The two parts of the same screen contradict each other.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** Pass `sessions = []` until Phase 4 and give NavSessions an empty state (or hide the group when empty). Alternatively, label the fixture session as a sample.

**10. Advisory: `themeScript` ignores the `defaultTheme` prop**
- **File:** `components/theme/theme-provider.tsx:33` and `:94`
- **Evidence:** The script always falls back to the system theme. `ThemeProvider({ defaultTheme })` uses the prop when nothing is stored.
- **Impact:** Any caller passing `defaultTheme="light"` gets a dark first paint, then a switch. There is no such caller today.
- **Confidence:** High.
- **Fix:** Remove the prop, or turn the script into a function of `defaultTheme`.

**11. Not worth changing (one-line fixes): stale comments**
- `components/design-system/catalog-navigation.tsx:114` says `SidebarMenuSkeleton` "picks its width with Math.random()". It now derives the width from `useId` (`sidebar.tsx:597-600`), so the `<Suspense fallback={null}>` wrapper is unneeded.
- `theme-provider.tsx:30` refers to `resolveTheme`, which does not exist.
- **Fix:** Update both comments, and drop the Suspense wrapper.

**12. Not worth changing: the modal lock also makes Next's body-level elements inert**
- **File:** `lib/quartz/portal.ts:58-80`
- **Evidence:** `inertOutside` makes every body child except the root container inert. That includes Next's `next-route-announcer` and, in dev, the dev overlay.
- **Impact:** Route announcements are muted while a modal is open. This is rare and acceptable.
- **Note:** The lock otherwise behaves correctly. Toasts, tooltips and popovers portal into the root container, so they stay usable above a modal. Nested and simultaneous closes balance the counter, and the lock is released in a layout effect before Base UI returns focus.

---

**Checked and found clean:**
- **SSR safety:** `use-mobile.ts` and the theme provider use `useSyncExternalStore` with server snapshots. There is no `window`, `localStorage` or `matchMedia` at render. Calendar dates are fixed (`new Date(2026, …)`), so they are allowed under `cacheComponents`.
- **Listener and timer cleanup:** `copy-button` (timer and requestAnimationFrame), `segmented-control` fonts listener, `code-block` scroll listener, `useScrollFade`, `useOffscreen`, sidebar keydown, and the media and storage subscriptions all clean up.
- **Toast managers:** Module-level managers are used on the client only. The separate managers for the anchored and bottom-start toasts avoid duplicate rendering.
- **Server→client props:** The `(app)` layout passes only the zod-parsed `session` (no cards). `DecisionCardPreview` is a server component and never renders `model_answer`.

I did not run the app, so findings 5 and 7 are from reading the code and were not observed in a browser.

Accessibility and input review of `git diff main...HEAD -- apps/web/src`. This was a read-only code review. Nothing was run in a browser, so every runtime claim below is marked as needing a check. Paths are relative to `/Users/ziaurrehmankhan/code/personal/session-coach/.claude/worktrees/design-system-landing-shell-57b973/apps/web/src/`. Every finding is introduced by the branch, because Quartz, the shell, the landing page and the gallery are all new files.

## Findings (most severe first)

**1. Important (close to Blocking). The highlighted menu row is the only focus indicator, and it is about 1.1:1 against the popup.**
- **Where:** `components/quartz/base.css:124-133` takes the ring away from `[role^=menuitem]` and `[role=option]`. `components/quartz/utilities.css:737` sets `outline: none;`. `components/quartz/utilities.css:744-746` sets `&[data-highlighted] { background-color: var(--surface-hover); }`, and `--surface-hover` is `oklch(var(--ink-c) / 0.05)` (`tokens.css:366`).
- **Contrast:** 5% ink on paper works out to about 1.12:1 in light and about 1.09:1 in dark. This is my estimate from the OKLCH lightness values, not a measurement.
- **Combobox:** focus stays in the input, so `focus-visible:focus-ring-inset` on the row never fires. On top of that, `select.tsx:33` gives the same wash to selected and to selected-plus-highlighted (`data-selected:bg-accent-wash data-selected:data-highlighted:bg-accent-wash`). So when the highlight lands on the chosen option, nothing on screen changes.
- **Impact:** a keyboard user cannot reliably see which row is current. This affects the theme toggle, the NavUser menu, every DropdownMenu and every Combobox. It fails WCAG 1.4.11 (non-text contrast of a state indicator). Forced-colors mode is handled correctly (`utilities.css:761-768`).
- **Confidence:** high on the code. The contrast figure is an estimate.
- **Fix:** add an inset ring to the highlighted row in the `menu-item` utility: `&[data-highlighted] { outline: var(--line-focus) solid var(--border-focus); outline-offset: calc(var(--line-focus) * -1); }`. Also stop giving selected-and-highlighted the same fill as selected-at-rest.

**2. Important. Links rendered through `<Button nativeButton={false} render={<Link/>}>` get `role="button"`.**
- **Where:** `app/(marketing)/page.tsx:49` ("See how it works"), `components/marketing/site-nav.tsx:37` ("Sign in"), `app/(app)/dashboard/page.tsx:46` ("Set up the CLI"), and the section nav in `app/design-system/page.tsx:70-76`.
- **Evidence:** Base UI 1.9 `internals/use-button/useButton.js:186` returns `isNativeButton ? { type: 'button' } : { role: 'button' }` with no exception for `<a href>`. Space then fires a click on keyup (lines 166-170).
- **Impact:** screen readers announce these navigation links as buttons and leave them out of the links list. This fails WCAG 4.1.2.
- **Confidence:** high. Confirm in the accessibility tree.
- **Fix:** use `<Link className={buttonVariants({ size: "lg" })}>`. `buttonVariants` is already exported from `components/ui/button.tsx:84`.

**3. Important. The landing nav links are gone below `md` and nothing replaces them.**
- **Where:** `components/marketing/site-nav.tsx:25`: `<ul className="hidden items-center gap-8 md:flex">`.
- **Impact:** on phones, and at 400% zoom (320 CSS px), How it works, Pricing, Support and Dashboard cannot be reached from the header. This fails WCAG 1.4.10 (no loss of function on reflow).
- **Confidence:** high.
- **Fix:** add a small menu, a Sheet or a DropdownMenu, under `md`, or let the list wrap or scroll instead of hiding it.

**4. Important. The active sidebar item has no `aria-current`.**
- **Where:** `components/nav-main.tsx:28` passes `isActive`. `components/ui/sidebar.tsx:505-520` only puts `active` into render state, which becomes `data-active`. The highlight is `data-active:bg-selected data-active:font-medium` (`sidebar.tsx:469`).
- **Impact:** screen reader users are not told which page they are on. The current page is shown only visually.
- **Confidence:** high.
- **Fix:** in `SidebarMenuButton`, add `"aria-current": isActive ? "page" : undefined` to `mergeProps`.

**5. Important. `/design-system` has no `<main>` landmark.**
- **Where:** `app/design-system/page.tsx:47` wraps the page in a `<div className="mx-auto flex ...">`. There is no route layout, and the root layout `<body>` has no `<main>`.
- **Impact:** there is no main landmark to jump to on a very long page.
- **Confidence:** high.
- **Fix:** change that `div` to `main`.

**6. Important (needs a runtime check). Sticky bars can cover anchored headings and focused elements.**
- **On `/design-system`:** the condensed page header is `sticky ... h-(--topbar)`, which is 56px (`components/ui/page-header.tsx:123-140`). The sections only reserve `scroll-mt-6`, which is 24px (`components/design-system/gallery-section.tsx:16`). Jumping to `#inputs` and similar anchors leaves the `h2` under the bar.
- **In the app shell:** the header is `sticky top-0 z-10 h-(--topbar)` (`app/(app)/layout.tsx:19`).
- **Missing everywhere:** there is no `scroll-padding-top` anywhere in `app` or in the Quartz CSS.
- **Impact:** focused elements and anchor targets can be hidden. This risks failing WCAG 2.4.11.
- **Fix:** use `scroll-mt-[calc(var(--topbar)+--spacing(6))]` on the sections, and set `scroll-padding-top: var(--topbar)` on the scroll root of any page with a sticky bar.

**7. Important. Field descriptions and errors are not linked to their controls.**
- **Where:** `components/ui/field.tsx:105-118` (`Field` is a plain div), `field.tsx:161` (`FieldDescription` is a `<p>` with no id) and `field.tsx:205-247` (`FieldError` is a `role="alert"` div with no id). The gallery at `components/design-system/catalog-inputs.tsx:123-128` sets `aria-invalid` on the input, but the error text is never linked to it.
- **Impact:** a screen reader on the field hears "invalid" but not why, and never hears the description. This fails WCAG 1.3.1 and 3.3.1 once real forms use these components.
- **Confidence:** high.
- **Fix:** build on Base UI `Field` (Root, Label, Description, Error), which links these automatically. Otherwise generate ids and set `aria-describedby` on the control.

**8. Advisory (needs a runtime check). The unlayered base focus rule overrides the layered `focus-ring-inset` and `focus-ring-ink` utilities.**
- **Where:** `base.css:127-133` sits outside `@layer base`, which closes at line 96. `focus-ring-inset` and `focus-ring-ink` are Tailwind `@utility` rules (`utilities.css:378-405`), so they live in a layer. Unlayered rules always win, so on `:focus-visible` the ring comes out as ink at +2px offset.
- **Affected:**
  - Tabs (`tabs.tsx:119`)
  - Sidebar menu buttons (`sidebar.tsx:466`)
  - Segmented items (`segmented-control.tsx:219`)
  - Toggle-group items, pressed toggles (`toggle.tsx:16`)
  - Calendar days (`calendar.tsx:183-187`)
  - Item rows

  Row and option roles are excluded from the base rule, so Select options and timeline rows are fine.
- **Impact:** the ring is drawn outside where the design meant inside. It can be clipped by `overflow-auto` (SidebarContent, `sidebar.tsx:362`). On ink-filled cells next to other ink cells (calendar range, `spacing=0` groups), an ink ring disappears against them. This is partial loss, not total.
- **Confidence:** medium.
- **Check:** focus a tab and read `getComputedStyle(el).outlineOffset`. Expect `-2px`; I suspect it will be `2px`.
- **Fix:** add `!important` inside those two utilities, or add `[class*="focus-ring-"]` to the `:not()` exclusion. Do not move the base rule into `@layer base`: Button, Toggle, Switch and Checkbox use `outline-none` and depend on the unlayered rule to show their ring.

**9. Advisory. The toast stack and exit slide ignore reduced motion.**
- **Where:** `components/ui/toast.tsx:66` sets `[transition:transform_500ms_var(--ease-out-quint),...]`. Lines 70-78 use `translateY(150%)` and swipe transforms. There is no `motion-reduce:` variant in the file. The global reduced-motion block (`app/globals.css:105-118`) only resets the tw-animate variables and the sheet.
- **Impact:** with reduced motion on, toasts still slide 150% and scale.
- **Confidence:** high.
- **Fix:** add `motion-reduce:[transition:opacity_150ms]` to the root, and fade on exit instead of translating.

**10. Advisory. SidebarTrigger does not expose its state.**
- **Where:** `components/ui/sidebar.tsx:252-271` has an sr-only "Toggle sidebar" label but no `aria-expanded` and no `aria-controls`.
- **Impact:** on desktop, screen reader users cannot tell whether the sidebar is open.
- **Fix:** add `aria-expanded={isMobile ? openMobile : open}`.

**11. Advisory. NavUser's name is noisy, and a dead link is focusable.**
- **NavUser:** the trigger name is built from the avatar initial plus the text, so it reads "L Learner Not signed in" (`components/nav-user.tsx:28-30`). Mark the avatar `aria-hidden`.
- **Feedback link:** `components/app-sidebar.tsx:44` has `{ title: "Feedback", url: "#" }`, a focusable link that goes nowhere. Hide it until there is a target.

**12. Not worth changing. The theme toggle is 32px.**
- **Where:** `components/theme/theme-toggle.tsx:33` uses `size="icon"`, which is 32px. The outline "key" variant has no coarse-pointer `touch-target`.
- **Why it can stay:** it meets WCAG 2.5.8 AA (24px) and has nothing close beside it.
- **Also fine:** the name `Theme: ${theme}` is acceptable, and `useSyncExternalStore` avoids a hydration mismatch.

## Checked and fine
- **Decorative pieces:** `HandUnderline` is `aria-hidden`, and its reduced-motion version swaps to static strokes through `motion-reduce:hidden` / `motion-reduce:inline`. The ruled SVG and the back cards of the stack are `aria-hidden` and contain nothing focusable.
- **Hero:** entrances use `motion-reduce:animate-none`.
- **Page basics:** `lang="en"` is set; nothing needs a `dir`.
- **Landing:** it has header, nav (`aria-label="Main"`), main and footer.
- **Headings:** `/dashboard` has one `h1` (PageHeaderTitle). `/design-system` goes h1, then h2 per section, then h3 per specimen.
- **Copy button:** it has an `aria-label`, a polite `role="status"` region and `aria-busy`.
- **Status:** the live region renders only when `live` is set. `role="img"` on the arc and `stat.tsx:283` both carry labels.
- **Breadcrumb:** it sets `aria-current="page"`.
- **Text contrast:** `text-3` (5.5–5.8:1) and the success and warning badge pairs (label tokens on their washes, at least 4.5:1 in both themes) clear AA by the values documented in `tokens.css`. I did not measure these myself.
- **Form names:** the gallery inputs, switches, sliders and segmented controls all have names.
- **Color scheme:** `color-scheme` follows the theme.

---
Not done: no browser check of the accessibility tree, focus or contrast, and no screen reader run. Findings 6 and 8 need DevTools to confirm. I did not check drawer swipe alternatives or tabs arrow-key behaviour beyond trusting Base UI, and I did not review Calendar keyboard handling.

## Motion regression review: zip vs merged `apps/web/src/components/ui`

**No Blocking regressions found.** Every micro-interaction on your list is still present with the same values:
- **Switch:** squash and stretch is intact. `--sx`/`--sy`, scale plus translate, `transition-[translate,scale,border-radius] duration-150 ease-out-quint`, and the motion-reduce reset are all there.
- **Tabs:** the indicator still slides on `--active-tab-*` with `ease-in-out-quint`. The panel still fades and slides by activation direction.
- **Toast:** stacking, swipe and the 150% exits are intact. The pulse moved from a class to `data-pulse=even|odd`. I checked that it lands on the Toast root.
- **Drawer:** swipe progress, the `.32,.72,0,1` overlay curve and the `.45,1.005,0,1.005` content curve are kept.
- **Checkbox:** blur plus scale is unchanged.
- **Dialog, alert-dialog, sheet:** enter/exit and nested stacking are kept.
- **Popover, menu, select, combobox, tooltip:** all keep `origin-(--transform-origin)` with animate-in/out.
- **Raw curve:** `cubic-bezier(.22,1,.36,1)` became `ease-out-quint` (or `var(--ease-out-quint)` in the toast transition shorthand) in toast and drawer. No raw copy is left under `ui/`.
- **globals.css:** the reduced-motion block matches zip `tokens.css:395-409` exactly.

Paths below: Z = `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-ziaurrehmankhan-code-personal-session-coach--claude-worktrees-design-system-landing-shell-57b973/605c7929-5046-44a2-9a58-97527d70dbba/scratchpad/zip/src/src_v2/components/ui/`. ### [Important] Press and hover timing switched from Tailwind's default to Quartz tokens on button, switch track, checkbox box, toggle, select and combobox
- **Scope:** introduced by the branch.
- **Evidence:** zip `Z/button.tsx:10` has `transition-[color,background-color,border-color,box-shadow,scale]` with no duration or ease, so Tailwind's default applies (150ms, `cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)`). Merged `M/button.tsx:19` has `transition-[...,scale] duration-(--dur-fast) ease-(--ease-step) active:duration-(--dur-press)`, which is 120ms, `(.2,0,0,1)`, and 60ms on press.
- **Same change elsewhere:**
  - Switch track: `Z/switch.tsx:38` vs `M/switch.tsx:42`.
  - Checkbox box: `Z/checkbox.tsx:11` vs `M/checkbox.tsx:22`.
  - Toggle: `Z/toggle.tsx:7` vs `M/toggle.tsx:14`.
  - Select and combobox triggers: `M/select.tsx:38` and `M/combobox.tsx:27`.
- **Impact:** the 0.98 scale is unchanged. The press now takes 60ms and the release 120ms, where both took 150ms on the default curve. You required "unchanged", so this is a deviation even if it is a deliberate Quartz choice.
- **Confidence:** HIGH.
- **Fix:** if the zip timing must win, remove `duration-(--dur-fast) ease-(--ease-step) active:duration-(--dur-press)` from the scale transition in `button.tsx:19`. Otherwise, record this as an intended deviation.

### [Advisory] Drawer reduced motion still moves to snap points and scales nested drawers
- **Scope:** introduced by the branch. Zip had no reduced-motion handling here.
- **Evidence:** `M/drawer.tsx:146` resets only the starting and ending states: `motion-reduce:data-starting-style:transform-none ... motion-reduce:data-ending-style:opacity-0`. The open-state transform at `:136`, `translate3d(var(--translate-x),var(--translate-y),0)_scale(var(--stack-scale))`, still transitions over 450ms.
- **Impact:** under reduced motion, a drawer that opens to a snap point animates from `none` to its snap offset. A parent drawer also still scales when a child drawer opens.
- **Confidence:** MEDIUM.
- **Fix:** add `motion-reduce:[--stack-step:0]`, or `motion-reduce:transition-[opacity]` while the drawer is not swiping. Swipe follows the finger and can stay.

### [Advisory] Nested-dialog dim is much stronger than in zip
- **Scope:** introduced by the branch.
- **Evidence:**
  - Dialog: zip `Z/dialog.tsx:39` uses `after:bg-foreground/5`. Merged `M/dialog.tsx:72` uses `after:bg-overlay`, which resolves to `oklch(ink / 0.24)` (`quartz/tokens.css:378`).
  - Alert dialog: zip `Z/alert-dialog.tsx:47` vs merged `M/alert-dialog.tsx:84`, same change.
- **Impact:** the recede animation still runs, but the parent dims to about 24% ink instead of 5%. The stacked look reads as a second backdrop.
- **Confidence:** HIGH that the value changed. Whether it was intended is unknown.
- **Fix:** keep it if intended. To match zip, use `after:bg-ink/5`.

### [Advisory] Tooltip arrow removed
- **Evidence:** zip `Z/tooltip.tsx:45` renders `<TooltipPrimitive.Arrow className="... rotate-45 ...">`. Merged `M/tooltip.tsx` has no Arrow.
- **Impact:** motion is unchanged at `M/tooltip.tsx:59`, but the arrow no longer travels with the zoom. This looks like a Quartz skin choice. The anchored toast arrow is still there at `M/toast.tsx:225`.
- **Confidence:** HIGH.
- **Fix:** none needed if intended.

### [Advisory] A read-only switch still squashes on hover and press
- **Scope:** introduced by the branch. `readOnly` and `pending` are new props.
- **Evidence:** `M/switch.tsx:131` resets the squash only for pending and disabled: `group-data-pending/switch:[--sx:1]! ... group-data-disabled/switch:[--sx:1]!`. There is no `group-data-readonly/switch` reset.
- **Impact:** a `readOnly` switch that is not pending stretches its thumb on press but never moves. The motion promises an action that cannot happen.
- **Confidence:** MEDIUM. It assumes Base UI sets `data-readonly` on the root, which the root classes at `:46` already rely on.
- **Fix:** add `group-data-readonly/switch:[--sx:1]! group-data-readonly/switch:[--sy:1]!` at `:131`.

### [Advisory] `transition-all` in sidebar
- **Evidence:** `M/sidebar.tsx:286` has `"absolute inset-y-0 z-20 hidden w-4 transition-all ease-linear ..."`. Sidebar has no zip counterpart.
- **Impact:** any change to this rail's properties (inset, width, background) now transitions. It is the only `transition-all` under `ui/`.
- **Confidence:** HIGH.
- **Fix:** narrow it to the properties that actually change. If nothing on the rail should animate, remove it.

### [Advisory, known deviation] Dropdown, select and combobox now animate
- **Evidence:** zip `Z/dropdown-menu.tsx:40` and `:134`, `Z/select.tsx:85` and `Z/combobox.tsx:109` all end with `animate-none!`, which cancelled the animation. Merged `M/dropdown-menu.tsx:16-17` (`popupMotionClass`) is shared by `M/select.tsx:25` and `M/combobox.tsx:151`, and none of them has `animate-none!`.
- **Kept from zip:** `duration-100`, the slide/fade/zoom classes, and select's `data-[align-trigger=true]:animate-none` (`M/select.tsx:26`).
- **Status:** your decision is pending. The submenu inherits the motion through `DropdownContent` (`M/dropdown-menu.tsx:169-185`).

### [Not worth changing] Layout properties animated, inherited from zip
- **Tabs indicator:** `transition-[translate,width,height]` at `M/tabs.tsx:99`, same as `Z/tabs.tsx:88`.
- **Alert-dialog nested stack:** `transition-[top,scale]` at `M/alert-dialog.tsx:84`, same as `Z/alert-dialog.tsx:47`. Dialog uses translate for the same effect.
- **Toast:** `height_150ms` at `M/toast.tsx:66`, same as `Z/toast.tsx:56`.
- **Drawer:** `transition-[transform,height,...]` at `M/drawer.tsx:136`, same as `Z/drawer.tsx:110`.
- **Progress:** `transition-[width]` at `M/progress.tsx:48`, same as `Z/progress.tsx:38`. The branch added `motion-reduce:transition-none`.
- **Collapsible (new, no zip counterpart):** animates `h-(--collapsible-panel-height)` at `M/collapsible.tsx:87`. This is Base UI's standard pattern and has `motion-reduce:transition-none`.
- None of these was a transform in zip, so none is a regression.

### [Not worth changing] globals.css reduced-motion reset ignores `data-force-reduced-motion`
- **Evidence:** `app/globals.css:105` resets the `--tw-enter-*`/`--tw-exit-*` variables only under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)`. Quartz's `motion-reduce` variant (`quartz/utilities.css:69-75`) also honours a `[data-force-reduced-motion]` wrapper.
- **Impact:** inside such a wrapper, switch and drawer would show reduced motion, but popups would still slide and zoom. Nothing in the app's `.tsx` sets the attribute today, so this has no effect yet.
- **Fix, if a preview starts using it:** add `[data-force-reduced-motion] [data-slot]` to the selector.

### [Not worth changing] Stale comment
- `app/globals.css:58` says "Switch needs a per-property delay no utility can express". The switch comment at `M/switch.tsx:133-147` says there is no delay. The stale text was copied from zip `tokens.css:34`.

(check), spinner.tsx, data-list.tsx, timeline.tsx. Read each file's exports before using it.

CREATE, in apps/web/src/components/app/ (one component per file, no barrel/index files, server components unless they need state):

7. `score-badge.tsx` – ScoreBadge for explain-back score 0/1/2 (0 = missed, 1 = partly, 2 = explained; check docs/plans for the exact words, else TODO(zia)) using success/warning/default tokens; never color-only (always a word).
